### Below the gate

Three locales keep meaning-correct wording below 85% because the reviewers found no faithful shorter phrasing. Their measurements and the alternatives they rejected are in the reviewers' own words under each locale below.

- **`eu` (Basque).** Set at 80% on iPhone and 82% on Android phone. Held down by `shot_overlay_hero` ("Denbora errealeko / kolonia-estrategia"): each line of the genre term is long enough to cap the set at about 80%, it matches the `headline` and `ios_subtitle` term as the glossary asks, and the only shorter option, "RTS", would be unnatural. `shot_overlay_fair_deal` is also below 85%; the shorter wordings that fit read as "internal purchase" or "buying the game".
- **`sw` (Swahili).** Set at 72% on iPhone and 73% on Android phone, held down by `shot_overlay_fair_deal` ("Bila matangazo wala / ununuzi mchezoni"). Both facts cannot fit in about 16 characters a line; the only measured wordings at 81% or more were ungrammatical or used bare "purchases", which the glossary bans.
- **`ta` (Tamil).** Set at 76% on iPhone and 78% on Android phone, held down by `shot_overlay_fair_deal` ("இன்-ஆப் வாங்கல், / விளம்பரம் இல்லை"). Any line holding the unsplittable phrase for "in-app purchases" tops out at about 77–80%; reaching 85% would mean splitting or dropping it.

### Per-locale caption results

#### ar — Arabic (caption pass)

Gate: **passed**. Catalog: [ar.md](../translations/ar.md). Review record: [reviews/ar.json](../translations/reviews/ar.json).

Fields changed in the pass (6): `shot_overlay_battle`, `shot_overlay_economy`, `shot_overlay_fair_deal`, `shot_overlay_hero`, `shot_overlay_maps`, `shot_overlay_opponents`.

Fields the reviewer changed: none.

**Errors fixed (0)**

- None reported.

**Residual concerns (4)**

- hero uses 'استراتيجية آنية' where the rest of the catalog says 'في الوقت الحقيقي'. I kept 'آنية' because it is an established, shorter term for real time, and the longer phrase would pull the Android phone set (now 86%) toward the 85% floor.
- opponents uses 'يديرهم الحاسوب' where the long-form fields use 'يتحكم بهم الحاسوب'. Both mean computer-controlled rivals and neither reads as an AI agent. I kept the shorter one.
- fair_deal uses 'لا شراء داخل اللعبة' (in-game buying), not the store's exact 'عمليات شراء داخل التطبيق'. It still clearly means in-game purchases and matches the facts in trailer_line_4.
- ai_coplay 'العب مع وكيل ذكائك الاصطناعي' is compact rather than the catalog's 'الخاص بك' form. I left it unchanged because it must match trailer_line_ai_coplay, and this pass covers only shot_overlay_\* fields.

#### bg — Bulgarian (caption pass)

Gate: **passed**. Catalog: [bg.md](../translations/bg.md). Review record: [reviews/bg.json](../translations/reviews/bg.json).

Fields changed in the pass (4): `shot_overlay_fair_deal`, `shot_overlay_hero`, `shot_overlay_maps`, `shot_overlay_territory`.

Fields the reviewer changed: none.

**Errors fixed (0)**

- None reported.

**Residual concerns (3)**

- shot_overlay_hero 'Стратегия в реално време' drops 'colony' from 'Real-time colony strategy'. It was kept because the glossary requires the same genre term as headline/ios_subtitle/play_short_description, and a colony version such as 'Колониална стратегия в реално време' (34 chars) is over the 28-char target and 'колониална' suggests colonialism.
- shot_overlay_fair_deal 'Без реклами и игрови покупки' breaks as 'Без реклами и / игрови покупки', so the conjunction 'и' ends line 1. Moving it with a no-break space ('Без реклами / и игрови покупки') drops the iPhone set to 83%. The two-sentence form matching the new English ('Без реклами. Без игрови покупки') drops it to 73%. 'игрови покупки' (in-game purchases) keeps the in-app meaning but is not the catalog's store term 'покупки в приложението', which is too long for a caption (36+ chars).
- The iPhone set sits exactly at the 85% threshold, so there is no headroom if any caption gets longer.

I tried 'ইন-অ্যাপ কেনাকাটা নেই', but it failed the gate: it was 83% on google-play-phone, marked SMALL, and broke inside 'ইন-|অ্যাপ' on the iPhone. So I reverted it. #### ca — Catalan (caption pass)

Gate: **passed**. Catalog: [ca.md](../translations/ca.md). Review record: [reviews/ca.json](../translations/reviews/ca.json).

Changed to '8 rivals dirigits per l'ordinador' ('8 rivals directed by the computer'). It is unambiguous, matches the catalog's 'oponent controlat per l'ordinador' construction, and 'rivals' is already used in the catalog. It breaks naturally as '8 rivals dirigits / per l'ordinador' and leaves the set size unchanged at 87% iPhone and 89% Android. I tried '8 rivals controlats per l'ordinador' first, but it dropped the set to 83/84% (SMALL), so I rejected it.

- shot_overlay_territory breaks as 'Tria / on expandir-te', a lone verb on the first line. That is a verb/complement boundary, not a split tight phrase, so I left it.
- shot_overlay_build 'Tu decideixes. Ells construeixen' uses 'ells' for the colony's workers, as the field map allows. trailer_line_1 uses 'Tu manes. Ells construeixen.' instead. This pass covers captions only, so I did not align the two.

#### cs — Czech (caption pass)

Gate: **passed**. Catalog: [cs.md](../translations/cs.md). Review record: [reviews/cs.json](../translations/reviews/cs.json).

Fields changed in the pass (6): `shot_overlay_ai_coplay`, `shot_overlay_build`, `shot_overlay_economy`, `shot_overlay_fair_deal`, `shot_overlay_hero`, `shot_overlay_maps`.

Fields the reviewer changed: none.

**Errors fixed (0)**

- None reported.

**Residual concerns (5)**

- shot_overlay_build 'Vy rozhodujete. Kolonie staví.' does not match trailer_line_1 'Vy rozhodujete. Oni staví.'. The field map asks for the same wording in both. The overlay version is the better one, because the guidance prefers a noun for 'they', so trailer_line_1 should probably change to match it. That field is outside this pass.
- shot_overlay_ai_coplay 'Hrajte spolu se svým AI agentem' does not match trailer_line_ai_coplay 'Hrajte se svým AI agentem.'. The overlay is clearer: 'spolu' marks playing on the same side, and a bare 'hrát s' can mean playing against. The trailer line is outside this pass.
- shot_overlay_hero 'Koloniální RTS' uses the genre term RTS. ios_subtitle and headline use 'Strategie v reálném čase', and the field map asks hero to use the ios_subtitle term. I kept it because it carries the full meaning (colony + real-time strategy) and RTS is a Czech search term already in ios_keywords. Also, 'Koloniální strategie v reálném čase' (35 chars) would need a 20-character line and pull the whole set well below 92%. A native reviewer may still prefer 'Strategie v reálném čase', which matches but drops colony.
- shot_overlay_maps '23 map, nebo náhodné' leaves the noun out after 'náhodné' and has a comma before 'nebo'. Both are acceptable caption style: the comma reads as an afterthought, and 'map' just before makes clear these are game maps. 'nebo náhodné mapy' would be more explicit but would lengthen line 2 and shrink the set.
- shot_overlay_fair_deal 'Bez reklam a nákupů ve hře' uses 'nákupy ve hře', which matches trailer_line_4. fair_deal_line uses the store term 'nákupy v aplikaci'. Both clearly mean in-game purchases. The split into two sentences ('Bez reklam. Bez nákupů ve hře') would need a 17-character line and lower the set size, so I kept the 'a' form. It breaks cleanly before 'a', so no single-letter word is left at a line end.

#### es — Spanish (caption pass)

Gate: **passed**. Catalog: [es.md](../translations/es.md). Review record: [reviews/es.json](../translations/reviews/es.json).

Fields changed in the pass (6): `shot_overlay_battle`, `shot_overlay_economy`, `shot_overlay_fair_deal`, `shot_overlay_hero`, `shot_overlay_maps`, `shot_overlay_opponents`.

Fields the reviewer changed (4): `shot_overlay_maps`, `shot_overlay_hero`, `shot_overlay_economy`, `shot_overlay_battle`.

**Errors fixed (5)**

- shot_overlay_maps: "23 mapas, o aleatorios" could be read as "23 maps, or (they are) random" because the noun the adjective refers to is left out after the comma. Changed to "23 mapas, o uno aleatorio" (23 maps, or a random one), so "uno" clearly stands for a game map. It breaks after the comma: "23 mapas, / o uno aleatorio".
- shot_overlay_hero: the renderer broke inside the phrase "en tiempo real" ("Estrategia en / tiempo real"). Added U+200B after the space, following the id.md precedent, so it now breaks "Estrategia / en tiempo real". The visible text is unchanged and still matches ios_subtitle and headline.
- shot_overlay_economy: the break left the preposition "a" hanging at the end of the first line ("Alimenta a / tu colonia"). A U+200B hint now gives "Alimenta / a tu colonia".
- shot_overlay_battle: "Lucha por / el territorio" split the prepositional phrase. A U+200B hint now gives "Lucha / por el territorio".
- Tried and reverted: shot_overlay_fair_deal "Sin anuncios ni compras en la app" matches the catalog's store term but dropped the whole set to 81% on iPhone and 83% on Android phone (SMALL). I kept "Sin anuncios ni micropagos", which clearly means purchases inside the game and breaks between the two facts.

**Residual concerns (5)**

- shot_overlay_fair_deal uses "micropagos" (microtransactions), not the store term "compras dentro de la app". To Spanish players it clearly means in-game purchases, but strictly it is narrower than "in-app purchases". The store term does not fit at 85%.
- shot_overlay_hero drops "colony" ("Estrategia en tiempo real"). "Estrategia de colonias en tiempo real" (37) is over the 32 target and would need a 22-character line. The current caption matches ios_subtitle and headline, as the guidance asks.
- shot_overlay_opponents "8 rivales CPU" is terse gamer shorthand. The catalog elsewhere says "rivales controlados por la CPU", which is 32 characters and would split badly. I kept it because it reads as CPU-controlled rivals, not as an AI agent or as rivals of a computer.
- Outside my scope (not a shot_overlay field): trailer_line_4 "Sin anuncios ni compras." uses a bare "compras", which the guidance forbids. It should say in-app purchases, for example "Sin anuncios ni compras dentro de la app." It is also meant to carry the same facts as shot_overlay_fair_deal.
- shot_overlay_hero, economy and battle now contain U+200B after a space, used only to place the line break. It is invisible, and check passes with it.

#### eu — Basque (caption pass)

Gate: **not passed**. Catalog: [eu.md](../translations/eu.md). Review record: [reviews/eu.json](../translations/reviews/eu.json).

Fields changed in the pass (5): `shot_overlay_ai_coplay`, `shot_overlay_economy`, `shot_overlay_fair_deal`, `shot_overlay_hero`, `shot_overlay_maps`.

Fields the reviewer changed: none.

**Errors fixed (0)**

- None reported.

**Residual concerns (5)**

- The legibility gate still fails, so fit exits 1. Two captions are marked SMALL: hero 'Denbora errealeko / kolonia-estrategia' (80% on iPhone, 82% on Android phone) and fair_deal 'Ez iragarki, / ez jokoko erosketa' (80% and 82%). The whole set renders at 80% on iPhone and 82% on Android phone; the smallest tablet set is 76% (amazon-tablet, set by hero).
- Hero cannot reach 85% without losing meaning. On two lines, any line of about 17 or more characters measures about 80%, and both 'Denbora errealeko' (17) and 'kolonia-estrategia' (18, one word) are that long. 'Denbora errealeko / estrategia' and 'Estrategia, / denbora errealean' measured the same 80-81%. The genre term is kept to match headline and ios_subtitle ('Denbora errealeko estrategia'), as the glossary asks. The only shorter option, 'RTS', would be unnatural here.
- fair_deal: 'Ez iragarki, ez barne-erosketa' measured at 85% or more, but 'barne-erosketa' can read as 'internal or domestic purchase', so it was rejected under the in-app-purchase rule. 'ez joko-erosketa' can read as 'buying the game', which is a price meaning. 'Ez iragarki, ez erosketa jokoan' measured 79%, no better. Hero holds the set at 80% whatever fair_deal says, so the clearer 'jokoko erosketa' (in-game purchase) was kept. A shorter wording would only remove fair_deal's SMALL flag.
- Measurement caveat: in scratch test catalogs, the per-caption 'on its own' figures came out inflated (91-96%) when another caption pulled the set far below 80%. Only the figures from realistic sets were relied on.
- Back-translations, all judged accurate and natural, so nothing was changed. build 'Zuk erabaki, haiek eraiki' = You decide, they build. economy 'Eduki kolonia elikatuta' = Keep the colony fed. battle 'Borrokatu lurragatik' = Fight for the land. territory 'Aukeratu nora hedatu' = Choose where to expand. maps '23 mapa, edo ausazkoak' = 23 maps, or random ones. opponents 'Ordenagailuko 8 aurkari' = 8 computer opponents, the same term the catalog uses in feature_opponents. ai_coplay 'Jokatu zure AI agentearekin batera' = Play alongside your AI agent. All line breaks fall at natural phrase boundaries.

#### fr — French (caption pass)

Gate: **passed**. Catalog: [fr.md](../translations/fr.md). Review record: [reviews/fr.json](../translations/reviews/fr.json).

Fields changed in the pass (4): `shot_overlay_build`, `shot_overlay_fair_deal`, `shot_overlay_maps`, `shot_overlay_opponents`.

Fields the reviewer changed (2): `shot_overlay_fair_deal`, `shot_overlay_maps`.

**Errors fixed (2)**

- shot_overlay_fair_deal: 'Sans pub ni achats intégrés' broke as 'Sans pub ni / achats intégrés'. That split one coordinated clause and left 'ni' hanging at the end of line 1, where the field map requires the break between the two facts. Changed to 'Ni pub, ni achats in-app', which breaks as 'Ni pub, / ni achats in-app'. Both facts are kept, and 'in-app' keeps the in-game-purchase meaning. Two-fact versions using 'achats intégrés' ('Ni pub, ni achats intégrés' and 'Sans pub. Sans achats intégrés') make the second line too wide: they reach 83% on their own, or force a break inside the clause.
- shot_overlay_maps: '23 cartes ou cartes aléatoires' repeated 'cartes' and broke after the conjunction ('23 cartes ou / cartes aléatoires') instead of after a comma. It was also the widest caption and held the iPhone set at 89%. Changed to '23 cartes, ou aléatoires' (back-translation: '23 maps, or random ones'). It breaks after the comma, and 'aléatoires' agrees with 'cartes', so the random ones are clearly game maps. It has no 'generators' and no 67. Set size rose to 93% on iPhone and 95% on Android phone.

**Residual concerns (5)**

- fair_deal now uses 'achats in-app'. This is the common French gamer and press shorthand allowed by the field map's 'shorter form' clause, but it is an anglicism and differs from the store term 'achats intégrés' used in trailer_line_4 and elsewhere. Under a strict France or Quebec terminology policy, the only option that passes the gate is 'Sans pub ni achats intégrés' (89%), and its break falls after 'ni' rather than between the two facts.
- shot_overlay_build ('Vous décidez. La colonie bâtit') differs from trailer_line_1 ('Vous décidez. Ils bâtissent.'), although the field map says they should share the same text. I did not change it because trailer fields are outside this pass. Someone should align trailer_line_1 to the overlay wording, which already follows the 'use the colony noun' guidance.
- shot_overlay_hero ('Stratégie en temps réel') leaves out 'colonie', like ios_subtitle. 'Stratégie de colonie en temps réel' is 34 characters, over the target of 32, and its natural break produces a 20-character first line, so I kept the shorter genre term.
- shot_overlay_opponents ('8 adversaires informatiques') is correct French for computer opponents but shorter than the catalog's 'adversaires contrôlés par l'ordinateur', which runs to 39 characters and does not fit. It does not read as an AI agent. It breaks only between the noun and its adjective, since breaking after '8' would leave the number alone on a line.
- I reviewed battle, economy, territory and ai_coplay and left them unchanged. Each matches the English meaning and the catalog's terms: battle matches defense_heading, territory matches map_body, and ai_coplay matches trailer_line_ai_coplay.

Each either fell below 85%, split 'in-app vásárlás' ('Reklám és in-app / vásárlás nélkül', 87%), or would read as a positive claim ('In-app vásárlás / és reklám nélkül', 90%: the bold first line says 'In-app purchases'). I kept the current text as the best trade-off.

- fair_deal sets the size for the whole set: 'Uten kjøp i appen' (17 chars) is the longest line and holds the iPhone set at exactly 85%. The required store term 'kjøp i appen' leaves no shorter faithful wording. 'Uten ...' also matches trailer_line_4, which is out of scope for this pass, so I kept it rather than switching to the more catalog-common 'Ingen reklame. Ingen kjøp i appen' (33 chars, over the target).
- shot_overlay_maps '23 kart, eller tilfeldige' uses an elliptical plural adjective that refers back to 'kart'. It is grammatical and clearly means game maps, but 'tilfeldige kart' would be more explicit; I kept the shorter form for set size and because it matches the English 'or random ones'.

The only other break, at the space, would leave '8 แบบ' alone on line 2. Of the two, the current break reads better. The wording matches feature_opponents.

## Caption touch-up — 2026-11-24

### Method

- **Why.** After the [round 2 final review](../assets/storefront/review/round-2-final.md) the caption renderer stopped ending the first line of a two-line caption with a word of four letters or fewer (review M3; [renderer README](../assets/storefront/tools/README.md#how-a-caption-is-fitted)), and the mobile gallery order became hero, battle, fair_deal, build, economy, territory, maps, with the opponents slide dropped (M1, M2). Under the new break rule some captions broke badly or set their locale smaller. This touch-up re-tuned the `shot_overlay_*` captions of 19 locales: `bg`, `de`, `el`, `eo`, `fil`, `hu`, `id`, `ms`, `nl`, `pt-BR`, `pt-PT`, `ro`, `sr-Cyrl`, `sv`, `tr`, `sw`, `sl`, `eu`, `ta`.
- **Order and tests.** `asset_text.mobile_gallery_order` and `desktop_gallery_order` in `translations/platforms.json` (and the Microsoft caption rows numbered from the desktop order) follow the reviewed gallery order; `translations/tests/test_storefront.py` was updated to match (76 tests, OK). `opponents` keeps its translated caption fields but is in no gallery.
- **Legibility gate.** As in the [caption pass](#caption-pass--2026-11-24): `screenshots.py fit --locale <code> --min-scale 0.85`, which fails when either phone-portrait set (`apple-iphone-6.9`, `google-play-phone`) is set below 85% of full caption size. Tablet sets are reported but do not count.
- **Editor and independent reviewer per locale.** Each locale had a caption editor and then a separate caption reviewer, both editing only `shot_overlay_*` fields. The reviewer back-translated the captions, ran the gate and re-recorded the hash-bound review with `storefront.py review-record` (identities `storefront-v2-caption-editor-<code>` and `storefront-v2-caption-reviewer-<code>`). This is AI editing with independent AI review, not native-speaker validation.
- **How this section was made.** It is generated by script from the workflow's structured results: per locale, the editor's changes (field, before, after, reason) and the reviewer's fields changed, errors fixed, residual concerns, gate result and fit summary. Their text is verbatim except for three mechanical changes: `*` escaped, the absolute local repository path shortened to a repository-relative one, and local scratchpad paths replaced with "(session scratchpad)". "Gate" is the reviewer's final result. "Changed in touch-up" counts the `shot_overlay_*` fields that differ from the previous commit, measured from the catalogs; the script confirmed that no other field changed in any of the 19 catalogs. Editor "before" and "after" values are as the editor reported them, including any notes in brackets.

### Results

- **Gate.** 15 of 19 locales pass the 85% gate. Below it: `eu`, `pt-PT`, `sw`, `ta`.
- **Reviews re-recorded.** 19 of 19; `storefront.py check --require-review` validates all 45 canonical locales.
- **Changed in the touch-up** (editor and reviewer): 28 fields in 18 locales. No catalog change: `eu`.
- **Fields changed by reviewers.** 7 in 5 locales.
- **Errors fixed by reviewers.** 8 entries in 6 locales.
- **Residual concerns.** 62 entries across 19 locales.

### Below the gate

4 locales keep meaning-correct wording below 85% because the editor and reviewer found no faithful shorter wording. The one-line summaries here are the steward's digest of the journal; the reviewers' own measurements, rejected alternatives and fit summaries are verbatim under each locale below.

- **`eu` (Basque).** Set at 80% on iPhone and 82% on Android phone, unchanged; the catalog was not edited. Held by `shot_overlay_hero` ("Denbora errealeko / kolonia-estrategia"): the line "Denbora errealeko" alone caps the set at 80%, and the genre term must match `ios_subtitle`. `shot_overlay_fair_deal` is also at 80%; the wordings that would fit ("app-erosketa", "jokoan erosi", "barne-erosketa") were judged less natural or ambiguous, and would not lift the set past the hero cap anyway.
- **`pt-PT` (Portuguese — Portugal).** Newly below the gate: set at 80% on iPhone and 83% on Android phone, held by `shot_overlay_fair_deal` ("Sem anúncios / ou compras in-app"). Under the new break rule every faithful wording with a clean break measured 80% or below; the only break near 89% ends line 1 on the conjunction "ou", which the rule forbids. The overlay now says "compras in-app" where the rest of the catalog says "compras na app".
- **`sw` (Swahili).** Set at 66% on iPhone and 68% on Android phone (72% and 73% in the caption pass; 64% and 66% under the new break rule before this touch-up), held by `shot_overlay_fair_deal` ("Bila matangazo, / bila ununuzi mchezoni"). "ununuzi mchezoni" alone is about 16 characters, the most a line can hold at 85%; shorter forms leave a bare "purchases" (banned by the glossary) or use "bila malipo", which reads as "free of charge".
- **`ta` (Tamil).** Set at 76% on iPhone and 78% on Android phone, held by `shot_overlay_fair_deal` ("ஆப்பில் வாங்கல், / விளம்பரம் இல்லை"). The editor reached 82% / 84% by fusing the negation onto "ads" only, which read as asserting that the game has in-app purchases; the reviewer restored the standalone இல்லை that negates both. No wording that keeps both facts negated reaches 85%.

### Per-locale touch-up results

#### bg — Bulgarian (caption touch-up)

Gate: **passed** (editor: not passed). Catalog: [bg.md](../translations/bg.md). Review record: [reviews/bg.json](../translations/reviews/bg.json).

Fields changed in the touch-up (3): `shot_overlay_battle`, `shot_overlay_fair_deal`, `shot_overlay_territory`.

Fields the reviewer changed: `shot_overlay_fair_deal`.

**Editor changes (2)**

- `shot_overlay_territory`: "Решете къде да се разширите (broke as "Решете / къде да се разширите", 66% on google-play-phone)" → "Решете къде ​да се разширите (breaks as "Решете къде / да се разширите")". The new rule refused to end line 1 on the 4-letter "къде", so the whole 27-character tail went onto line 2 and dragged the Google Play and F-Droid phone sets down to 66%. A U+200B after "къде" splits it at the natural clause boundary. The Google Play phone set is now at 85% and the tablets at 90% or more.
- `shot_overlay_battle`: "Борете се за земята (broke as "Борете / се за земята")" → "Борете се ​за земята (breaks as "Борете се / за земята")". The old break split the reflexive verb "Борете се" from its particle. A U+200B before "за" keeps the verb whole and gives two balanced 9-character lines.

**Errors fixed by the reviewer (1)**

- shot_overlay_fair_deal "Без реклами и игрови покупки" broke as "Без реклами / и игрови покупки" under the new rule and set the whole apple-iphone-6.9 set at 83% (gate exit 1). It is now "Без реклами и покупки в игри" ("No ads and no in-game purchases"). It breaks as "Без реклами / и покупки в игри", between the two facts, and the iPhone set is at 86%, Google Play phone at 88%. Gate exit 0.

**Residual concerns (3)**

- fair_deal now says "покупки в игри" (in-game purchases, in the generic sense) instead of the more idiomatic compound "игрови покупки", which can only reach 83% on iPhone. The meaning is intact and the in-app-purchase sense stays (never bare "purchases"). A native editor might still prefer "игрови покупки" at 83%.
- I tested other fair_deal wordings and all scored lower on iPhone. "Без реклами. / Без микроплащания" (the trailer_line_4 wording) gave 70%. "Без реклами / и микроплащания" gave 79%. "Без реклами, / без игрови покупки" gave 74%. So the overlay and trailer_line_4 ("Без реклами. Без микроплащания.") use different words for the same fact, which the field map allows.
- hero "Стратегия в реално време" leaves out "colony" to match ios_subtitle. The full "Колониална стратегия в реално време" is 35 characters, over the 28 target. I left it as it was.

**Reviewer fit summary**

The bg gate passes (exit 0). apple-iphone-6.9 is at 86% and google-play-phone at 88%; the smallest tablet set is 90% (amazon-tablet, set by territory). No caption is marked SMALL and nothing is listed under worth a look. Line breaks: hero \"Стратегия / в реално време\"; battle \"Борете се / за земята\" (a U+200B keeps \"Борете се\" together); fair_deal \"Без реклами / и покупки в игри\"; build \"Вие решавате. / Те строят.\"; economy \"Изхранвайте / колонията\"; territory \"Решете къде / да се разширите\" (a U+200B keeps \"да се разширите\" together); maps \"23 карти / или случайни\". Back-translations: real-time strategy / fight for the land / no ads and no in-game purchases / you decide, they build / feed the colony / decide where to expand / 23 maps or random ones. All match the English meaning, with no price words, no ALL CAPS, and digits kept. storefront.py check prints nothing for bg before and after the review was recorded in translations/reviews/bg.json. The only edited file is translations/bg.md (fair_deal only).

#### de — German (caption touch-up)

Gate: **passed** (editor: passed). Catalog: [de.md](../translations/de.md). Review record: [reviews/de.json](../translations/reviews/de.json).

Fields changed in the touch-up (1): `shot_overlay_maps`.

Fields the reviewer changed: none.

**Editor changes (1)**

- `shot_overlay_maps`: "23 Karten oder Zufallskarten" → "23 Karten oder zufällige". Under the new break rule this caption became '23 Karten / oder Zufallskarten'. The 18-character second line set the whole phone set to 81% on iPhone and 83% on Google Play. 'oder zufällige' is shorter and is a natural German way to say 'or random ones'. 'zufällige' refers back to 'Karten', so the random ones still read as game maps. The break falls after '23 Karten', which is the clause boundary.

**Errors fixed by the reviewer (0)**

- None reported.

**Residual concerns (2)**

- shot_overlay_fair_deal uses the singular 'Kein In-App-Kauf' (no in-app purchase). German stores usually say 'In-App-Käufe' (plural). The plural forms did not fit: I tested 'Werbefrei. Ohne In-App-Käufe', which matches trailer_line_4, and it dropped the iPhone set to 79% and the Google Play set to 81%. 'Keine In-App-Käufe' is wider still. I put the editor's wording back. It still clearly means in-app purchases.
- shot_overlay_maps '23 Karten oder zufällige' leaves 'Karten' out of the second half, the same way the English says 'or random ones'. It is grammatical and clearly means random game maps. 'Zufallskarten' is clearer, but it forced the break '23 Karten / oder Zufallskarten' and capped the set at 81%.

**Reviewer fit summary**

The fit gate exits 0. The iPhone 6.9 set is at 88% (tightest), the Google Play phone set at 90%, and the smallest tablet set at 85%. No caption is marked SMALL and nothing is listed under 'worth a look'. The breaks: hero 'Koloniestrategie / in Echtzeit', battle 'Kämpfe / ums Land', fair_deal 'Keine Werbung. / Kein In-App-Kauf', build 'Du entscheidest, / sie bauen', economy 'Ernähre / deine Kolonie', territory 'Wähle, / wohin du wächst', maps '23 Karten / oder zufällige'. None of them leaves a conjunction, article or preposition at the end of line 1, and none splits a verb or the in-app phrase. Back-translations: real-time colony strategy; fight for the land; no ads, no in-app purchase; you decide, they build (the same wording as trailer_line_1); feed your colony; choose where you grow; 23 maps or random ones. All match the English meaning, with no price words, no capitals for emphasis and digits kept. Hero matches ios_subtitle. I tested one alternative fair_deal caption, reverted it, and left the file as the editor wrote it. Validation for translations/de.md printed nothing for de, both before and after the review was recorded.

#### el — Greek (caption touch-up)

Gate: **passed** (editor: passed). Catalog: [el.md](../translations/el.md). Review record: [reviews/el.json](../translations/reviews/el.json).

Fields changed in the touch-up (1): `shot_overlay_territory`.

Fields the reviewer changed: none.

**Editor changes (1)**

- `shot_overlay_territory`: "Επιλέξτε πού θα επεκταθείτε" → "Επεκταθείτε όπου θέλετε". The new rule moved the break to 'Επιλέξτε / πού θα επεκταθείτε'. That left a long second line, and google-play-phone fell to 78% (SMALL). The new wording means 'expand wherever you choose' and breaks evenly as 'Επεκταθείτε / όπου θέλετε'. It is shorter, and nothing dangles at the end of line 1.

**Errors fixed by the reviewer (0)**

- None reported.

**Residual concerns (2)**

- shot_overlay_territory 'Επεκταθείτε όπου θέλετε' (expand wherever you like) is a slight adaptation of 'choose where to expand'. The literal 'Επιλέξτε πού θα επεκταθείτε', which matches the description's 'Αποφασίστε πού θα επεκταθείτε', breaks as 'Επιλέξτε / πού θα επεκταθείτε' under the new rule and was SMALL at 78% on google-play-phone. The editor's wording keeps the meaning that the player chooses the direction, so I accepted it.
- shot_overlay_fair_deal uses the colloquial short form 'in-app αγορά' rather than the store term 'αγορές εντός εφαρμογής'. The field map allows a shorter form that clearly means purchases inside the game, and the full store phrase does not fit the 36-character limit together with the ad clause. Line 2 starts with 'ή' ('Καμία διαφήμιση / ή in-app αγορά'), which still keeps the break between the two facts.

**Reviewer fit summary**

The fit run for el exits 0. apple-iphone-6.9 is at 89% and google-play-phone at 91%. Before the editor's change google-play-phone was at 78%, set by territory. Tablets are at 88% or above (smallest google-play-tablet-7, set by fair_deal). No SMALL captions and no bad breaks were flagged. Back-translations of the gate's breaks: hero 'Στρατηγική / RTS με αποικίες' (RTS colony strategy, same term as ios_subtitle); battle 'Πολεμήστε / για τη γη' (fight for the land); fair_deal 'Καμία διαφήμιση / ή in-app αγορά' (no ad or in-app purchase); build 'Εσείς ορίζετε. / Αυτοί χτίζουν' (you decide. they build, same as trailer_line_1); economy 'Ταΐστε / την αποικία' (feed the colony); territory 'Επεκταθείτε / όπου θέλετε' (expand wherever you like); maps '23 χάρτες / ή τυχαίοι' (23 maps or random ones; the masculine plural agrees with 'maps'). #### fil — Filipino (caption touch-up)

Gate: **passed** (editor: not passed). Catalog: [fil.md](../translations/fil.md). Review record: [reviews/fil.json](../translations/reviews/fil.json).

Fields changed in the touch-up (2): `shot_overlay_fair_deal`, `shot_overlay_territory`.

Fields the reviewer changed: `shot_overlay_fair_deal`, `shot_overlay_territory`.

**Editor changes (2)**

- `shot_overlay_fair_deal`: "Walang ads o in-app purchase" → "Walang ads​ o in-app purchase". Under the new rule the caption broke as 'Walang ads o in-app / purchase', which split 'in-app purchase' and set the iPhone phone set to 73% and Google Play to 75%. Putting a U+200B after 'ads' gives 'Walang ads / o in-app purchase'. That keeps the store term whole, keeps 'o' with the phrase it joins, and raises the caption to 83% on iPhone and 85% on Google Play and F-Droid.
- `shot_overlay_territory`: "Piliin kung saan lalawak" → "Piliin saan lalawak". 'Piliin / kung saan lalawak' was 83% on its own and fell below 85% on google-play-phone. Dropping 'kung' is common in casual spoken Tagalog and keeps the meaning 'choose where to expand'. The caption now breaks 'Piliin / saan lalawak' and fits at 85% or more.

**Errors fixed by the reviewer (2)**

- fair_deal 'Walang ads​ o in-app purchase' broke as 'Walang ads / o in-app purchase' and set the iPhone 6.9 set at 83%, below the 85% gate (exit 1). The 'Walang ads o / in-app purchase' break would reach 91%, but it leaves the conjunction 'o' dangling at the end of line 1, and every comma or 'walang ... walang' form came out between 75% and 83%. Replaced with 'Walang ads​ o pagbili sa app' (back-translation: 'No ads or purchases in the app'). It breaks 'Walang ads / o pagbili sa app', keeps the two facts on separate lines and keeps the in-app-purchase meaning (it is close to Google Play's Filipino 'in-app na pagbili'). iPhone 91%, Google Play phone 94%.
- territory 'Piliin saan lalawak' had dropped the complementizer 'kung'. That reads like texting shorthand, not store copy. Restored the grammatical 'Piliin kung saan​ lalawak' ('Choose where to expand'), with a U+200B so it breaks 'Piliin kung saan / lalawak' ('Choose where / to expand'). Without the U+200B it broke 'Piliin / kung saan lalawak' and set Google Play phone at 77%; with it the caption is 94% and does not limit the set.

**Residual concerns (4)**

- fair_deal now says 'pagbili sa app' while trailer_line_4, fair_deal_line, short_pitch and shot_caption_fair_deal use the loanword 'in-app purchase'. The meaning is the same and the caption stands alone, but the wording differs across the catalog. Keeping the loanword would leave the iPhone set at 83%, or force a dangling 'o' break.
- hero stays 'Real-time strategy' with no 'kolonya'. It matches ios_subtitle and headline as the glossary requires. Adding 'ng kolonya' would put an 18-character first line on 2-line slides and drop the set to about 80%.
- build 'Pasya mo. Sila ang gagawa' uses 'gagawa' ('will make/do it') where the trailer has 'magtatayo' ('will build'). It is acceptable for 'they build it'; 'magtatayo' would make line 2 longer.
- maps 'o mga random' depends on ellipsis to mean random maps. That is natural in Filipino and Taglish and mirrors English 'ones'.

**Reviewer fit summary**

The fit run with --min-scale 0.85 exits 0, with no SMALL captions and no "worth a look" items. iPhone 6.9: every caption at 91%. Google Play phone: 94%. Tablet sets: 100% at smallest. Line breaks: Real-time / strategy; Ipaglaban / ang lupa; Walang ads / o pagbili sa app; Pasya mo. / Sila ang gagawa; Pakainin / ang kolonya; Piliin kung saan / lalawak; 23 mapa, / o mga random. storefront.py check prints nothing for fil, before and after review-record. Edited file: translations/fil.md

Versions that read more naturally but were too wide: 'Sem anúncios nem compras no app' (71%), 'Sem anúncios. Sem compras no app' (71%), 'Sem anúncios e compras no app' (84%) and 'e microtransações' (81%). The singular 'Sem anúncio e compra no app' scored 89% but was rejected because 'compra' can read as the verb 'buys'.
- `shot_overlay_battle`: "Lute pelo território (no-break space between 'pelo' and 'território')" → "Dispute o território". At the higher type size the old caption needed two lines. The only break left 'Lute', a 4-letter word, at the end of line 1, which the renderer flagged as a relaxed short-word break. 'Dispute o território' breaks cleanly as 'Dispute / o território'. It matches the listing's own wording ('dispute território com rivais') and still means fighting rival colonies for the land.

**Errors fixed by the reviewer (0)**

- None reported.

**Residual concerns (4)**

- The fair_deal caption 'Sem anúncios e compras in-app' (no ads and in-app purchases) uses the anglicism 'in-app'. Every other pt-BR field, including trailer_line_4 'Sem anúncios nem compras no app.', uses Google Play's pt-BR term 'compras no app'. I tested 'Sem anúncios e compras no app': the wider glyphs put the iPhone 6.9 set at 84% and the gate failed (exit 1), so I reverted it. 'Compras in-app' is a common, clear Brazilian term for in-app purchases, so it is acceptable here. The difference is there because of width only.
- The field map asks for a comma or two sentences between the two facts. A comma version ('Sem anúncios, sem compras ...') makes line 2 at least 18 characters, which falls below 85%. So 'e' joins them, and the line break still falls between the two facts. 'Sem A e B' reads naturally to native speakers as 'without either'.
- The hero caption 'Estratégia em tempo real' (real-time strategy) drops 'colony'. The field map allows that ('keep colony where it fits'), and the caption matches ios_subtitle. 'Estratégia de colônia em tempo real' would push the set well below 85%.
- The build caption 'Você decide. Eles constroem.' (you decide, they build) uses the masculine plural 'Eles' for the creatures on screen. The field map allows this, and it is identical to trailer_line_1. Writing 'A colônia constrói' (the colony builds) would not fit.

**Reviewer fit summary**

The gate passes (exit 0, no SMALL captions, nothing under 'worth a look'). The tightest set is apple-iphone-6.9 at 86%, google-play-phone is at 88% and the smallest tablet set is at 90%, all limited by fair_deal. Line breaks: hero 'Estratégia / em tempo real' (real-time strategy), battle 'Dispute / o território' (fight for the territory), fair_deal 'Sem anúncios / e compras in-app' (no ads and in-app purchases), build 'Você decide. / Eles constroem.' (you decide, they build), economy 'Alimente / a colônia' (feed the colony), territory 'Escolha / onde expandir' (choose where to expand), maps '23 mapas, / ou aleatórios' (23 maps, or random ones). Every back-translation keeps the English meaning, with no price words, no ALL CAPS and digits kept as digits. The editor's text stays unchanged in translations/pt-BR.md: I tried 'compras no app', it fell to 84%, and I reverted it. The review is recorded, and the storefront.py check prints nothing for pt-BR.

#### pt-PT — Portuguese — Portugal (caption touch-up)

Gate: **not passed** (editor: not passed). Catalog: [pt-PT.md](../translations/pt-PT.md). Review record: [reviews/pt-PT.json](../translations/reviews/pt-PT.json).

Fields changed in the touch-up (3): `shot_overlay_battle`, `shot_overlay_fair_deal`, `shot_overlay_territory`.

Fields the reviewer changed: `shot_overlay_fair_deal`, `shot_overlay_battle`.

**Editor changes (1)**

- `shot_overlay_territory`: "Escolhe para onde expandir" → "Escolhe onde expandir". Under the new rule this broke as 'Escolhe / para onde expandir' and held the Google Play phone set at 78%. 'Escolhe onde expandir' means the same and is natural European Portuguese. It now breaks as 'Escolhe / onde expandir' and stops limiting the set.

**Errors fixed by the reviewer (2)**

- fair_deal 'Sem anúncios ou compras na app' was SMALL and set the whole phone set at 78% (iPhone) / 80% (Play). I changed it to 'Sem anúncios ou compras in-app', which breaks 'Sem anúncios / ou compras in-app' and raises the sets to 80% / 83%. 'compras in-app' is common Portuguese tech usage and still clearly means purchases inside the game.
- battle 'Luta pelo território' broke 'Luta pelo / território' once the set grew to 80%, leaving the contraction 'pelo' dangling (the gate listed it under worth a look). I changed it to 'Luta por território', which is natural and fits on one line in every phone set. No breaks are flagged worth a look now.

**Residual concerns (7)**

- The gate still exits 1. fair_deal alone holds the phone sets at 80% (apple-iphone-6.9) and 83% (google-play-phone / fdroid-phone). Tablets are at 86% or more.
- The limit is the width of line 2. The two facts need a negator plus 'compras na app' or 'compras in-app', and 'ou compras in-app' (17 chars) is the narrowest second line that keeps the meaning. Measured alternatives: 'Sem anúncios, / sem compras na app' 72%, 'Sem anúncios / nem compras in-app' 72%, 'Sem anúncios. / Sem compras na app' 71%, 'Zero anúncios, / zero compras na app' 71%, '... ou compras na app' 78%.
- The only break that would reach about 89% is 'Sem anúncios ou / compras na app', which ends line 1 on the conjunction 'ou' (forbidden).
- 'Sem anúncios e compras in-app' was rejected because the second line, 'e compras in-app', could be read as 'and in-app purchases'.
- Consistency: the overlay now says 'compras in-app', while the rest of the pt-PT catalog (trailer_line_4, the descriptions) and the Google Play PT store term say 'compras na app'. The field map allows a shorter form on the overlay. To undo this, put back 'Sem anúncios ou compras na app', which costs 2 to 3 points (78% / 80%).
- territory 'Escolhe onde expandir' (the editor's change, breaking 'Escolhe / onde expandir') is accepted. 'para onde expandir' would be slightly more formal, but 'onde expandir' is natural gamer Portuguese and matches pt-BR.
- Back-translations of the unchanged captions: hero 'RTS de colónias' = colony RTS, build 'Tu decides. Eles constroem.' = You decide. They build. (same as trailer_line_1), economy 'Alimenta a colónia' = Feed the colony, maps '23 mapas, ou aleatórios' = 23 maps, or random ones. All are accurate and have no price words.

**Reviewer fit summary**

The pt-PT phone sets are at 80% (apple-iphone-6.9) and 83% (google-play-phone / fdroid-phone), and tablets at 86% or more. The gate exits 1, and fair_deal is the only caption that sets the size. The bad 'Luta pelo /' break in battle is fixed, no breaks are flagged worth a look, and storefront.py check prints nothing for pt-PT. The review is recorded in translations/reviews/pt-PT.json. Every fair_deal wording I measured with the right meaning and a clean break came out at 80% or below, so 85% looks out of reach without a dangling 'ou'. Only translations/pt-PT.md was edited.

#### sr-Cyrl — Serbian — Cyrillic (caption touch-up)

Gate: **passed** (editor: passed). Catalog: [sr-Cyrl.md](../translations/sr-Cyrl.md). Review record: [reviews/sr-Cyrl.json](../translations/reviews/sr-Cyrl.json).

Fields changed in the touch-up (2): `shot_overlay_battle`, `shot_overlay_fair_deal`.

Fields the reviewer changed: `shot_overlay_battle`.

**Editor changes (2)**

- `shot_overlay_fair_deal`: "Без реклама и куповина у игри" → "Без реклама и уплата у игри". Under the new rule the break moved to 'Без реклама / и куповина у игри'. That made line 2 17 characters wide and set the phone sets at 81% (iPhone) and 83% (Google Play). The old break after a dangling 'и' is no longer allowed. 'уплата у игри' (in-game payments) still clearly means paying inside the game, it is not a bare 'purchases', and it shortens line 2 so it fits. I rejected 'купња', which reads as Croatian, 'микроплаћања', which is not a common term, and comma versions like 'без куповина у игри', which only reached 72-75%.
- `shot_overlay_battle`: "Борите се за територију" → "Борите се​за територију (U+200B after 'се')". The new rule broke it as 'Борите / се за територију', which splits the verb from its reflexive particle 'се'. The zero-width space keeps them together: 'Борите се / за територију'.

**Errors fixed by the reviewer (1)**

- shot_overlay_battle: the editor had replaced the space between 'се' and 'за' with a U+200B ('Борите се​за територију'). On tablet sets the caption fits on one line, the U+200B is invisible there, and amazon-tablet rendered it as 'Борите сеза територију' with two words merged (confirmed with the fit tool). I changed it to 'Борите се ​за територију' (space, then U+200B). The phone and iPad break is still 'Борите се / за територију', which keeps the reflexive verb together, and every tablet set now shows 'Борите се за територију'.

**Residual concerns (3)**

- shot_overlay_fair_deal 'Без реклама и уплата у игри' means 'no ads and no in-game payments'. It keeps the in-app-purchase meaning, but it differs from trailer_line_4 and the description, which use 'куповина у игри' / 'куповина у апликацији'. The field map allows a shorter overlay form. I tested the alternatives with the renderer: 'Без реклама и куповина у игри' gives 81%/83% and 'Без реклама и плаћања у игри' gives 83%/84%, so both fail the 85% gate. Only 'уплата' passes, at 93%/94%, so I kept it. 'Уплата' also carries a bank-deposit or betting-stake sense, but on an RTS store page it reads as paying real money in the game.
- shot_overlay_hero 'RTS стратегија о колонији' ('RTS strategy about a colony') is slightly redundant, since RTS already includes 'strategy', and the 'о колонији' phrasing is a little loose. It is common gamer usage, it is accurate and it breaks cleanly as 'RTS стратегија / о колонији'. Wordings that drop the redundancy ran over the 36-character limit or lost 'colony', so I left it unchanged.
- shot_overlay_maps uses a comma before 'или', which Serbian normally leaves out. I kept it on purpose because the field map wants the break after the comma, and it reads fine as a caption.

**Reviewer fit summary**

fit --locale sr-Cyrl --min-scale 0.85 exits 0. The iPhone 6.9 set is at 93% and the Google Play phone set at 94%, with no SMALL captions and nothing listed under worth a look. The F-Droid phone set fit as well. Tablet sets are at 96-100%, all on clean single lines except the iPad, which uses two lines. Phone breaks: hero 'RTS стратегија / о колонији'; battle 'Борите се / за територију' (U+200B hint); fair_deal 'Без реклама / и уплата у игри'; build 'Ви одлучујете. / Они граде.'; economy 'Храните / колонију'; territory 'Ви бирате / куда се ширите' (U+200B hint); maps '23 мапе, / или насумичне'. Back-translations: real-time (RTS) colony strategy; fight for the territory; no ads and no in-game payments; you decide, they build; feed the colony; you choose where you expand; 23 maps, or random ones (the adjective agrees with 'maps', so it means game maps). No price words, no all-caps, digits kept. The storefront.py check printed nothing for sr-Cyrl before and after the review was recorded (translations/reviews/sr-Cyrl.json).

#### ta — Tamil (caption touch-up)

Gate: **not passed** (editor: not passed). Catalog: [ta.md](../translations/ta.md). Review record: [reviews/ta.json](../translations/reviews/ta.json).

Fields changed in the touch-up (1): `shot_overlay_fair_deal`.

Fields the reviewer changed: `shot_overlay_fair_deal`.

**Editor changes (1)**

- `shot_overlay_fair_deal`: "இன்-ஆப் வாங்கல், விளம்பரம் இல்லை" → "ஆப்பில் வாங்கல், விளம்பரமில்லை". The old caption held every Tamil phone set at 76% (iPhone) and 78% (Google Play). Its widest line was 'விளம்பரம் இல்லை', and 'இன்-ஆப் வாங்கல்,' was almost as wide. The new caption joins the second line by normal sandhi into 'விளம்பரமில்லை'. It also says in-app purchase in plain Tamil, 'ஆப்பில் வாங்கல்' (purchases in the app). That keeps the in-app meaning without the wide transliterated 'இன்-' prefix. The comma stays so the line reads as two items (in-app purchases, ads) that share one 'none'. Without it the caption could read as 'no in-app-purchase ads'. The set is now 82% on iPhone and 84% on Google Play. The break falls after the comma and there are no 'worth a look' warnings.

**Errors fixed by the reviewer (1)**

- shot_overlay_fair_deal: the editor's "ஆப்பில் வாங்கல், விளம்பரமில்லை" fused the negation onto 'ads' only, so it read as "In-app purchases, no ads", which asserts that the game has in-app purchases. Changed it to "ஆப்பில் வாங்கல், விளம்பரம் இல்லை" ("in-app purchases, ads: none"). A noun list followed by a standalone இல்லை negates both items. This is the same label structure the catalog uses in shot_caption_fair_deal. It breaks at the comma: "ஆப்பில் வாங்கல், / விளம்பரம் இல்லை".

**Residual concerns (4)**

- The gate still fails. fair_deal sets the whole set at 76% on apple-iphone-6.9 and 78% on google-play-phone, below the 85% target. Every other caption fits at 82% or more on its own. The editor's version reached 82%/84%, but only because the fused 'விளம்பரமில்லை' (no ads) changed the meaning.
- I found no wording that keeps both negated facts and reaches 85%. With both facts fused, 'விளம்பரமில்லை, ஆப்பில் வாங்கலில்லை' is 34 characters, over the 32-character limit. The neither/nor '-ஓ ... இல்லை' forms say it most clearly but measured only 66-68%. The ads-first list 'விளம்பரம், ஆப்பில் வாங்கல் இல்லை' measured 75% and splits the in-app phrase across the break. Shorter forms such as 'ஆப் வாங்கல்' (app purchase) or 'இன்-ஆப்ஸ்' (in-apps) lose the in-app-purchase meaning or could be read as a price claim.
- A reader could still read the first line alone as positive for a moment. The comma list plus a single இல்லை is standard Tamil label style and matches the catalog, but it is less explicit than the neither/nor form. The neither/nor form would cost about 9 more points of type size.
- maps 'சீரற்றது' is singular ('a random one'). The plural 'சீரற்றவை' fits the English 'random ones' better, but it drops maps to 77% on its own and would become a second bottleneck. I kept the singular, which still means you play one random map.

**Reviewer fit summary**

After the fix, uv run screenshots.py fit --locale ta --min-scale 0.85 exits non-zero. apple-iphone-6.9 sets every caption at 76% and google-play-phone at 78%; fair_deal is the only SMALL caption, and the smallest tablet set is 75%. Breaks: hero 'நிகழ்நேரக் / குடியிருப்பு உத்தி' (real-time colony strategy), battle 'நிலத்துக்காகப் / போரிடுங்கள்' (fight for the land), fair_deal 'ஆப்பில் வாங்கல், / விளம்பரம் இல்லை' (no in-app purchases, no ads), build 'முடிவு உங்களது. / கட்டுவது அவை' (the decision is yours; they build), economy 'குடியிருப்புக்கு / உணவிடுங்கள்' (feed the colony), territory 'எங்கு விரிவது? / உங்கள் தேர்வு' (where to expand? your choice), maps '23 வரைபடங்கள், / அல்லது சீரற்றது' (23 maps, or a random one). No bad breaks and nothing under 'worth a look'. All captions back-translate correctly and are consistent with ios_subtitle and trailer_line_1/2. Validation (storefront.py check | grep ^ta) printed nothing before and after review-record. The review was recorded with exit code 0. Candidate test catalogs are in (session scratchpad).

#### tr — Turkish (caption touch-up)

Gate: **passed** (editor: passed). Catalog: [tr.md](../translations/tr.md). Review record: [reviews/tr.json](../translations/reviews/tr.json).

Fields changed in the touch-up (2): `shot_overlay_battle`, `shot_overlay_maps`.

Fields the reviewer changed: none.

**Editor changes (2)**

- `shot_overlay_maps`: "23 harita ya da rastgele harita" → "23 harita veya rastgele biri". It was the only caption below the target, at 71% on iPhone and 73% on Android phone, and it pulled the whole set down with it. 'veya rastgele biri' means 'or a random one': 'biri' points back to 'harita', so the random ones still clearly mean game maps. It is close to the English 'or random ones'. It now breaks '23 harita / veya rastgele biri' and the set fits at 85% on iPhone and 88% on Android phone. I tried 'ya da rastgele' (95%) and 'ya da rastgelesi' (93%) and rejected both: the first leaves out what is random, and the second is colloquial.
- `shot_overlay_battle`: "Toprak için savaşın" → "Toprak için ​savaşın". At the new set size this caption wraps to two lines. The new rule broke it as 'Toprak / için savaşın', which cuts the postposition 'için' off from its noun. A U+200B after 'için' keeps 'Toprak için' together, so it breaks 'Toprak için / savaşın'. The visible text is unchanged.

**Errors fixed by the reviewer (0)**

- None reported.

**Residual concerns (3)**

- shot_overlay_maps 'veya rastgele biri' ('or a random one'): 'biri' here refers back to 'harita', the way 'kırmızı biri' means 'a red one'. A reader could still take it as 'one of the 23, picked at random'. The clearer 'veya rastgele harita' was tested on a scratch copy and drops the set to 74% (iPhone) and 76% (Google Play), so the editor's wording stays.
- shot_overlay_fair_deal uses 'oyun içi alım' (in-game purchase), which matches trailer_line_4. The long fields say 'uygulama içi satın alma'. Both are standard, but there are two terms in the catalog.
- Captions outside the 7 mobile roles (opponents, ai_coplay) were not reviewed.

**Reviewer fit summary**

The fit gate exits 0. Every caption on apple-iphone-6.9 (the tightest set) is at 85%, Google Play phone at 88% and the tablets at 91% or more, with nothing marked SMALL. Breaks: 'Gerçek zamanlı / koloni stratejisi'; 'Toprak için / savaşın' (a U+200B keeps the noun and its postposition together); 'Ne reklam / ne oyun içi alım'; 'Karar sizin, / inşaat onlardan'; 'Koloniniz / aç kalmasın'; 'Yayılacağınız / yeri seçin'; '23 harita / veya rastgele biri'. No line ends on a dangling word. Back-translations: real-time colony strategy; fight for the land; neither ads nor in-game purchases; the decision is yours, the building is theirs (matches trailer_line_1); don't let your colony go hungry; choose where you'll expand; 23 maps or a random one. All meet the accuracy rules, so no edits were made. Validation for tr was clean before and after the review was recorded.
